## Analysis Summary

### Comparison Snapshot

| Metric | Value |
|---|---|
| Runs covered | `114` |
| Scored events | `443` |
| Excluded `accidental` events | `7` |
| Overall success rate | `21.7% (96/443)` |
| `unpudo` success rate | `19.7% (85/431)` |
| `unparking` success rate | `91.7% (11/12)` |
| Ownership / handover failures | `82.1% (285/347)` |
| Short AV-attempt failures | `50.4% (175/347)` |
| Successful events with no driver accel help | `100.0% (96/96)` |
| Successful events with route change found | `96.9% (93/96)` |
| Successful gear alignment at anchor | `100.0% (96/96)` |

### Failure Profile

- The scored subset contains `347` failures out of `443` scored events. The dominant effective failure types are `not_av_owned` 62.2% (216/347), `completed_outside_av` 18.4% (64/347), `failed_to_unpudo` 14.1% (49/347).
- Ownership loss and handover breakage are the main theme: `285` of `347` failures fall into `not_av_owned`, `completed_outside_av`, `driver_completed_maneuver`, or `interrupted_handover`.
- Route-change evidence inside failed events is `found` in `297`, `not found` in `2`, and `unclear` in `48` cases.
- Source disengagement labels inside failed events are `failed_to_unpudo` 14.1% (49/347), `uncategorised` 1.4% (5/347), `failed_to_maintain_speed` 0.9% (3/347); anything else is failing from DBW / ownership / gear evidence rather than an explicit source disengagement label.
- Event-type split: `unparking` is stronger than `unpudo` in this sample: 91.7% (11/12) versus 19.7% (85/431).

### Success Behavior

- Successful events are distributed as `85` `unpudo` and `11` `unparking`.
- On successful attempts, route change is recovered in `93` of `96` cases and driver accelerator help is absent in `96` of `96` cases.
- Gear state stays aligned at the anchor in `96` of `96` comparable passes. Planned-indicator alignment is `25.0% (24/96)`, and controller-indicator alignment is `26.0% (25/96)`.
- Typical successful timing is median `success baseline -> event start` `15.16s`, median `success baseline -> first motion` `9.88s`, and median AV-owned duration `148.62s`.
- Typical successful maneuver shape: common actual gear at the anchor is `DRIVE_POSITION_V2_DRIVE`, common actual indicator is `INDICATORS_STATE_V2_OFF`, and median max AV speed is `4.78 m/s`.

### Written Assessment

This card covers `450` recorded events across `114` runs, but only `443` of them are scored AV-owned attempts. The remaining `7` `accidental` events are explicitly excluded from the success-rate denominator. Within the scored subset, the overall success rate is `21.7% (96/443)`. `unparking` is stronger than `unpudo` in this sample: 91.7% (11/12) versus 19.7% (85/431).

Across the scored failures, route-change evidence is `297` found / `2` not found / `48` unclear, so navigation context is usually present. The failure mix is led by `not_av_owned` 62.2% (216/347), `completed_outside_av` 18.4% (64/347), `failed_to_unpudo` 14.1% (49/347), while the excluded portion is limited to sub-`2s` accidental AV contact rather than true scored failures.

When this model succeeds, the behavior is consistent: route change is recovered in `93` of `96` successful events, driver accelerator help is absent in `96` of `96`, and gear alignment holds in `96` of `96` comparable passes. The typical successful event starts moving about `9.88s` after the earlier of route change and AV start, reaches a median max AV speed of `4.78 m/s`, and most often begins in gear `DRIVE_POSITION_V2_DRIVE` with indicator state `INDICATORS_STATE_V2_OFF`.
